## Document 039: 2026-11-18_［みずほ経済インサイト］「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる世界_a87be182

「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる世界
〜中国の「強すぎる供給⼒」がもたらす構造変化〜
【みずほ経済インサイト】

### Page 2

本資料の要旨
1
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
• 「チャイナショック」とは、中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える現象
• 「2.0」の特徴は、労働集約型産業での競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争力を高めていること
• 背景にあるのが中国特有の技術革新モデル。産業集積と規模の経済もあり、中国企業の競争力が格段に向上
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
• 欧州は輸出減少と輸入増加に直面。基幹産業でも中国製品の存在感が高まり、自動車産業を中心に競争が本格化
• ASEANは中国からの中間財調達が輸出産業にとって必須化。ただし、最終財を中心に域内産品との競合は強まる
• 中国製品の浸透を受け、欧州、ASEANとも貿易障壁措置を拡大。中国企業は貿易障壁を意識し、現地生産を志向

３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
• 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
• 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
• 中国が新興国においてAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築する「チャイナショック3.0」に発展する可能性も
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

2005年 2015年 2024年（％）
「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か――中国の「強すぎる供給力」が世界を変える現象
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える「チャイナショック2.0」が進行中
― 中国がWTO加盟を契機に生産・組立拠点として世界貿易体制に組み込まれたのが「チャイナショック1.0」
―「2.0」では、中国企業の競争力と供給力が飛躍的に向上し、先端産業分野で先進国との競合が激化
 「チャイナショック2.0」の特徴は、中国が労働集約型産業での輸出競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争
力を高めていること。中国企業は繊維・履物などの軽工業品からハイテク製品まで幅広い分野で輸出を伸ばしている
― 他方、「自立自強」政策による輸入代替の進展と内需の低迷で輸入が停滞。その結果、貿易不均衡が拡大
3
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

「チャイナショック2.0」はなぜ起きたのか――「中国式イノベーション」と製造業の生産性向上
 「チャイナショック2.0」の背景にあるのが「中国式イノベーション」。新興分野で中国の民営企業が躍進
― 「中国式イノベーション」とは、国家の戦略的主導と民間の市場志向が組み合わさった技術革新モデル（筆者定義）
― その土台にあるのが産業集積と規模の経済。こうした環境下、中国企業の技術力とコスト競争力が格段に向上
 中国の製造業は、政府の産業支援も背景に積極的な設備投資を行い、自動化と生産の効率化が急速に進展
― 産業用ロボットの新規導入台数（2025年）は35.4万台と世界（60.3万台）の過半を占め、稼働台数でも群を抜く
― 世界経済フォーラム（WEF）が認定するライトハウス（先進工場）拠点数で中国は最多。特に生産性の評価が高い

4
「中国式イノベーション」の特徴（筆者定義） 産業用ロボット稼働台数（左）とライトハウス拠点数（右）

製造業付加価値は世界の3割弱を占める規模。貿易構造が高度化しつつある様子も
 中国はすでに「世界の工場」の地位を確立。製造業付加価値（2025年）は4.8兆ドルと、世界の3割弱を占める規模
― 中国の輸出額（2025年）は3.77兆ドルと、世界の14.4％を占める。貿易黒字も1.18兆ドルと圧倒的な大きさ
― 輸出総額だけでなく、技術集約的なハイテク製品（航空、コンピューターなど）の輸出額も8,250億ドルと世界一
 加えて、近年は貿易構造が消費財（最終財）中心から中間財や資本財へと高度化しつつある様子もうかがえる
― より付加価値の高い製品の貿易特化係数（輸出入バランスから貿易品目の国際競争力を示す指標）が上昇傾向
5

軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械でも競争力向上

 中国は、軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械などでも競争力を向上させている
― HSコード別の貿易特化係数を見ると、2025年は2005年と比較して輸出競争力が大幅に向上している品目が多い
― 他方で、食品・原材料・素材の多くは輸入に頼る。「世界の工場」における需要拡大や食の多様化などが背景に
6

高
中国と先進国との輸出競合度が急上昇。自動車が最大の上昇要因
 中国と先進国の輸出類似度指数（ESI）は「チャイナショック2.0」で急上昇。両者の競合が強まっていることを示唆
― 日本は「1.0」と「2.0」の時期に、EUは2010年代中盤から「2.0」の時期にかけて、ESIが顕著に上昇
― 実際、EUは「2.0」の「震度」が大きく、中国製品の浸透による製造業の衰退が最も懸念されている地域
― 産業別に見ると、「2.0」では日本、EUともに自動車が最大の上昇要因になっていることが分かる
10
中国と先進国の輸出類似度指数（ESI） ESI上昇の産業別寄与度（コロナ禍前、コロナ禍後）

（注） SITC品目別3ケタ分類より算出
（出所） UN Comtradeより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

①チャイナショック2.0とは何か

### Page 12

「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
 「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。「自立自強」による輸入代替（国産化）の進展が背景に
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアを見ると、輸出が「1.0」の頃からほぼ一貫して上昇しているのに対し、輸入は2010
年代に入って頭打ちとなっていることが分かる
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小傾向にある

### Page 22

ASEAN5：国産品・中国産品の調達額変化の比較
ASEAN：一方、中国産品とASEAN域内産品の競合は強まる傾向
21
 素材や消費財、機械・電気機器等では、中国製品がASEAN域内産品を圧迫。最終需要で競合する分野が多い傾向
― 上記分野では中国産品の供給増と域内産品の供給減が同時に発生。中国産品が域内産品を上回って増加する例も
― 中間需要は3分野が該当。一方、最終需要では5分野が該当し、消費財等の部門でより強く競合している可能性を示唆

22
 EUは中国製品の流入拡大を背景に、貿易措置を積極化
― 対中貿易措置は2023年以降に増加。化学品や鉄鋼など幅広い品目が対象。2024年には中国製EVへの追加関税を発

 ASEANも貿易保護措置を拡大する傾向。鉄鋼製品等へのアンチダンピング・セーフガードのほか、越境ECへも対応を拡大
― ただし、対中貿易措置の比率は目立って高まらず。外交関係への配慮から、中国以外の地域も含む形で規制を強化

「チャイナショック2.0」の光と影――安価な製品供給の恩恵と先進国の競争力喪失リスク
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」は、世界市場に低価格と安定供給という恩恵をもたらしている
― 中国の輸出価格が相対的に低下する一方、輸出数量はハイペースで拡大しており、輸出ドライブが鮮明に
― 輸出ドライブによる安価な中国製品の供給拡大が世界の財価格を押し下げ、インフレ圧力の緩和にも寄与
 他方で、中国の高い輸出競争力は輸入国の産業競争力の低下と対中依存度の上昇を招いていると考えられる

― 先進国が中国企業の技術革新力とコスト競争力に圧倒され、得意としてきた先端産業で競争力を喪失するリスク
― 関税などで対中輸入を抑制しても、供給網を通じた中国依存は容易に低下せず。経済安保上の脆弱性は残存
26

### Page 28

「チャイナショック2.0」への対応――国内製造業の強化で対抗しつつ、中国製品の輸入も継続
 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
― 先進国は、戦略分野での産業基盤の強化や対中デリスキングを図りつつ、非機微分野で貿易・投資活動を維持
― 新興国は、脱中国化を図る供給網の一部を取り込んで国内産業の育成を企図。過剰な中国製品の流入には警戒も
 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
― 過剰生産能力への批判を一蹴し輸出拡大。一方で、関税回避や規制対応のため、海外での現地生産も積極展開
27
「チャイナショック2.0」への対抗と協調
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 29

EUは産業競争力の回復に向け、政策ロードマップを策定。IAAにより域内調達も強化へ
 第二期フォンデアライエン体制下で、EUは「競争力回復」に向けた政策のロードマップ（競争力コンパス）を策定
― （１）米中とのイノベーション格差是正、（２）脱炭素化と競争力強化の両立、（３）過剰な域外依存の軽減と安全保障の強
化を目指す。加えて、市場統合の深化や規制緩和等を内容とする（４）分野横断的取り組みを実行する構え
 競争力コンパス枠組みのもと、欧州は原産地規則の強化により、EVや鉄鋼産業等の域内調達を強力に推進する方針
― 2026年3月以降、欧州委員会は域内調達の拡大に向け、IAA（産業加速法）の立法化に着手
― IAAの現行案では、EVの公共調達や補助金等の支援に対し、域内調達・組立の要件を導入予定
（注） 7要件：①実質的な内国民待遇、②経済安全保障、③競争条件＋ILO、④パリ協定＋炭素価格、⑤企業行動・人権、⑥低炭素需要政策、⑦迂回防止
（出所） 欧州委員会（2025）“A Competitiveness Compass for the EU(factsheet)”、ほか各種資料より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州企業の行動にも変化の兆し。AIや再エネなど重点分野への投資が拡大

### Page 31

政策の概要国
• 2026年9月、輸入電気自動車への物品税引上げを決定。バッテリーEVのうち国内生産がなく、国産部品も使
用していない車両には2027年から物品税を10％→35％に引き上げる計画
• 現地部品の使用など、一定の国内調達や今後の調達率引き上げが見込まれるものについては、税率引き下
げも検討
タイ
• 四輪電気自動車（BEV）について国産化率規定を設置。政府は2026年までに40％、2030年以降に80％と段
階的な基準値引き上げを目指す
• 完成車輸入優遇の利用企業に、2027年末までに輸入台数以上の国内生産を義務付け
インドネシア
• 2026年7月、EVの国内生産・部品製造を支援するEVインセンティブ戦略（EVIS）を導入。交付された納税証
明書を各種納税に充当することで、企業が税務負担を軽減できる仕組み
• 一定の投資・生産要件を満たす企業に対し、投資額の最大40％を支給
• 国内製造・組立車両の生産実績に応じ、工場出荷価格の最大12％相当を支給
フィリピン
• 国内組立EVや関連部品の税金を2027年末まで一部免除（EV本体：物品税・販売税、部品：輸入関税）

• 完成車輸入に車両価格、およびエンジン容量の下限を設定（2026年7月～）マレーシア
• 国内で製造・組立を行い、生産台数等の要件を満たす企業に、国内生産できない自動車部品の輸入関税を
免除。環境対応車も対象ベトナム
ASEANは、対中関係深化のメリットを維持すべく、自動車を中心に現地生産を促す
30
 ASEANは現地生産要件の設置などで、中国との貿易関係や中国企業の投資流入を維持しながら、輸入品との競合による
域内産業への圧力緩和を目指す方向
― 2025年以降、ASEAN5各国は、自動車を中心に現地生産の促進や国内規定を徐々に厳格化
― 輸入車を中心にEVの浸透が進む中、現地生産化の促進により、中国企業による域内投資や地場サプライヤー・雇用へ
の波及効果を得る狙い
（注） 2026年11月6日時点の発表・報道等による
（出所） 各国政府発表、各種報道等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

おわりに：「チャイナショック2.0」とAI――製造業へのAI実装で一段と高まる中国の競争力
 中国は、AI技術で世界をリード。オープンウェイト戦略と国家主導のガバナンスで独自のAIエコシステムを構築しつつある
― 大規模言語モデルの性能は米国に肉薄。コストパフォーマンスで海外にも普及し、トークン消費量のシェアが急拡大
― 同時に、ヒューマノイドや自動運転といったフィジカルAI分野でも、技術開発と社会実装が急ピッチで進んでいる
 中国は今後、製造業へのAI実装によって生産性を向上、技術革新を加速させ、競争力を一段と高めるとみられる
― 中国政府は、経済社会の幅広い分野でAI導入を図る「AI＋（プラス）」政策を推進。「AI＋製造業」により研究開発設計、
生産、物流、サービスなど各バリューチェーンへの実装が進めば、「強すぎる供給力」がさらに強力に

## Document 069: 2026年10月经济数据点评：新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月经济数据点评：新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 16 日      
证 券研究报告•宏观简评报告     
数据点评 
新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压 
——2026 年 10 月经济数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院 
[Table_Summary]  摘要：工业生产方面，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月
回落 0.7 个百分点，低于市场预期。高技术制造业和装备制造业增加值分别增
长 17.6%、10.6%，其中高技术制造业创近五年来单月增速新高，AI相关电子
硬件、工业机器人、3D 打印设备等产品产量保持较快增长。同时，10 月规上
工业出口交货值增长 18.6%，外需仍是工业生产的重要支撑。整体工业生产呈
现总量放缓、结构分化的特征，高技术制造和出口链条景气度较高，传统周期
性行业修复相对滞后。固定资产投资方面，1-10 月全国固定资产投资同比下降
8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点，投资需求仍处于深度收缩区间。基建
投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资下降 5.1%，专项债发行节奏放缓、资金向实
物工作量转化存在时滞，整体托底效应尚未充分显现，但互联网和相关服务业
投资，以及交通运输、电力供应等重点领域投资仍保持正增长。随着专项债剩
余额度加快使用及“两重”项目建设推进，重点基建领域投资有望获得一定支
撑，但扭转整体投资下行趋势仍面临较大难度。房地产投资方面，1-10 月房地
产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点，地产仍是整体
投资的重要拖累。供给端施工、竣工面积单月同比分别下降 32.5%、39.1%，
降幅均有所扩大，新开工降幅有所收窄但仍处负增长区间；需求端商品房销售
面积、销售额分别下降 20.7%、20.5%，降幅小幅收窄，但销售修复尚未有效
传导至房企投资和施工活动。房企资金来源继续收缩，行业投资意愿和项目建
设规模仍然偏弱，预计年内地产投资仍处深度调整阶段，降幅收窄时点或推迟
至 2027 年。制造业投资方面，1-10 月制造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9
月扩大 0.8 个百分点，企业资本开支意愿仍然偏弱。高技术制造业投资增长
3.0%，电子电路、锂离子电池和集成电路制造业投资增速较快，科技产业投资
仍具韧性。“两新”政策继续支撑设备更新，设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，
占全部投资比重提升至 20.3%。但汽车、通用设备等装备制造行业投资明显走
弱，反映生产端韧性尚未转化为新增资本开支。消费方面，10 月社会消费品
零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，消费需求整体偏弱。
汽车类零售同比下降 21.4%，仍是社零增长的主要拖累；通讯器材、金银珠宝
等品类受前期高基数及金价波动影响，零售增速明显回落，家电、家具消费则
受低基数影响边际改善。服务消费相对更具韧性，1-10 月服务零售额增长
4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，汽车及地产相关消费能否改善、
以旧换新政策效应能否延续，仍是影响商品消费修复的重要因素，服务消费则
有望继续成为居民消费的重要支撑。 
 工业生产总量边际降温，科技制造与出口链条维持高景气。2026 年 10 月，全
国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点，低于
wind 一致预期的 5.32%。分行业看，采矿业、制造业、电力、热力、燃气及
水生产和供应业的工业增加值同比较上月均边际走弱，增速分别回落至 0.7%、
5.4%、4.4%。但制造业工业增加值同比相比近十年季节性水平略高，表明生
产端并未出现明显失速。结构上，10 月高技术产业、装备制造业工业增加值
同比分别增长 17.6%、10.6%。高技术产业增速创下近 5 年来单月最高增速，
其中电子工业专用设备制造、计算机外围设备制造、电子器件制造等行业增加
值分别增长 46.8%、44.7%、34.8%，显示电子信息产业链仍处于较高景气区
间。装备制造业 1-10 月累计增加值占全部规模以上工业比重为 37.9%，同比
提高 1.8 个百分点。细分行业中，10 月单月，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制

2
造业、专用设备制造业、石油和天然气开采业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他
运输设备制造业增加值同比分别增长 19.4%、11.5%、9.4%、9.2%，在主要
行业中增幅居前。金属制品业（8.6%）、食品制造业（8.6%）、医药制造业（5.9%）
增速均边际回升。非金属矿物制品业（-2.3%）、煤炭开采和洗选业（-4.4%）
同比降幅均小幅收窄。这反映出工业生产的改善并非全面扩散，传统周期性行
业仍受到需求不足、价格偏弱等因素制约，产业间景气分化较为明显。从产品
看，“人工智能+”带动产业链上下游产量较快增长，既体现在集成电路
（+21.9%）、电子元件（+15.2%）等核心硬件持续扩张，也体现在工业机器人
（+26.0%）、 3D 打印设备（+85.9%）等高端制造设备加快放量，以及地面通
信导航定向设备（+61.5%）等智能终端产品快速增长，反映出 AI产业发展正
由底层硬件向智能制造和多元化应用场景延伸。出口亦对工业生产形成正向带
动。10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，增速较上月加快 1.6

个百分点。装备制造业出口交货值增长 20.6%，增速较上月加快 3.4 个百分点，
其中，汽车、电气机械、电子行业出口交货值分别增长 52.2%、23.7%、15.3%，
对全部规上工业出口交货值增长的贡献率居各大类行业前三位，合计达
66.7%，说明出口对工业生产的拉动并未明显减弱，且支撑力量主要集中在装
备制造和电子产业链。综合来看，年内工业增加值增速或温和放缓，结构升级
趋势明确，高技术制造业和装备制造业是核心支撑。在强出口和稳投资政策发
力下，单月增速有望小幅回升，但难以扭转全年较前三季度小幅放缓的格局。
 整体投资降幅进一步扩大，重点基建领域维持较高景气。2026 年 1-10 月，全
国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分
点。根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月投资同比下降 17.84%，降幅较上月扩
大 8.16 个百分点。累计降幅扩大相对温和，但单月降幅明显加深，反映近期
投资下行压力仍在加大。基建投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资同比下降 5.1%，

降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点；根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月广义基建
投资同比下降 17.63%，降幅较上月扩大 7.15 个百分点。资金端来看，10 月
地方政府新增专项债发行 3280 亿元，同比涨幅收窄至 14.13%，发行进度
88.07%，慢于去年同期 2.03 个百分点。专项债发行节奏放缓，叠加前期资金
向项目建设转化存在时滞，基建投资尚未形成回升趋势。尽管广义基建投资整
体负增长，但重点领域投资仍保持较快增长。1-10 月互联网和相关服务业投资
同比增长 48.4%，增速较 1-9 月份加快 1.7 个百分点，反映数字基础设施建设
仍有较强投资需求；航空运输业、水上运输业投资均增长 12.5%，电力供应业
投资增长 9.9%，说明“两重”项目建设及“六张网”相关领域投资仍在推进。
往后看，在四季度财政资金集中拨付、重大项目加速形成实物工作量的支撑下，
11、12 月单月投资增速环比有望小幅改善，带动全年降幅较 1-10 月略有收窄，
但全年转正的概率较低。基建投资中部分结构性高增领域可能受低基数、项目

集中开工影响，持续性需观察。同时，专项债剩余额度在年内可能对基建形成
一定支撑，但不宜高估其扭转深度负增的能力。
 地产供需两端低位运行，投资意愿与在建规模仍偏弱。2026 年 1-10 月，全国
房地产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点；据历史数
据倒推测算，10月单月房地产开发投资同比下降 34.24%，降幅较上月扩大 7.04
个百分点，地产对整体投资的拖累有所加深，是投资端最大拖累项之一。供给
端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月房屋施工面积同比下降 32.5%，降幅较上
月扩大 13.9 个百分点，反映在建规模明显收缩；新开工面积同比下降 18.0%，
降幅收窄 8.1 个百分点，是供给端唯一边际改善项。新开工绝对增速仍深度负
增，且可能受低基数、项目集中开工影响，房企投资意愿仍未实质性修复；竣
工面积同比下降 39.1%，降幅扩大 2.4 个百分点，反映项目交付端压力或边际
上升。需求端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 20.7%，

降幅较上月收窄 1.9 个百分点；销售额同比下降 20.5%，降幅收窄 0.6 个百分
点。销售额降幅收窄幅度小于销售面积，意味着销售均价同比表现相对偏弱，
销售端的量价修复仍不充分。同时，销售端边际改善与投资、施工、竣工端恶
化形成背离。说明销售回款尚未有效转化为房企拿地、开工和施工强度，房企
流动性和投资意愿仍是核心约束。资金端，据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月
房地产开发资金来源同比下降 22.0%，降幅较上月进一步扩大。其中国内贷款、
自筹资金、定金及预收款分别下降 33.0%、22.0%、15.6%，资金链仍在全面

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
收缩。往后看，年内稳地产政策以公积金改革、核心城市限购优化、购房贴息
等需求端工具为主，供给端主要依赖收储去库存，年底地产投资降幅仍在寻顶，
降幅收窄时点或推迟到 2027 年。
 整体企业资本开支意愿偏弱，高技术制造投资稳健增长。2026 年 1-10 月，制
造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.8 个百分点；据历史数据倒推
测算，10 月单月制造业投资同比下降 9.32%，降幅较上月扩大 8.38 个百分点。
结合本月工业生产数据看，当前生产端的增长尚未充分转化为企业新增资本开
支，制造业投资意愿相对谨慎。分行业看，1-10 月高技术制造业投资同比增长
3.0%，其中电子电路制造业投资增长 59.6%，锂离子电池、集成电路制造业
投资分别增长 18.0%、13.8%；高技术服务业投资增长 5.9%，其中与人工智
能等新兴产业密切相关的信息服务业投资增长 20.5%，显示科技产业投资持续
向电子信息制造和人工智能相关服务领域集中。“两新”政策持续落地显效，

设备购置投资规模不断扩大。1-10 月，设备工器具购置投资同比增长 8.9%；
占全部投资的比重为 20.3%，比上年同期提高 3.2 个百分点。10 月单月，金属
制品业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业投资增速同比分别下降 8.5%和 2.5%，降
幅较上月分别收窄 8.2 和 6.8 个百分点。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设
备制造业、汽车制造业、通用设备制造业当月投资同比分别下降 0.2%、21.8%、
21.2%，降幅分别扩大 12.7、13.6、23.5 个百分点。装备制造业内部投资分化
加剧，终端需求偏弱、价格竞争加剧、产能利用率不足，以及前期抢出口和“以
旧换新”带来的需求前置，可能共同影响企业扩产意愿，促使企业资本开支趋
于谨慎。对比来看，10 月装备制造业工业增加值仍增长 10.6%，反映出当前
装备制造业呈现生产保持较强韧性、投资扩张意愿不足的特征，生产端与投资
端的背离进一步加深。往后看，高技术制造业投资动能有望延续，四季度制造
业投资单月同比有望转正，但全年增速明显转正难度较大；设备购置、电子电

路、锂电、集成电路和 AI 相关信息服务是主要支撑，汽车、通用设备和传统
制造或成为主要拖累。
 消费总量增长继续放缓，部分品类受到价格和低基数扰动。10 月单月，社会
消费品零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；扣除汽车后
的消费品零售额同比增长 2.5%，增速较上月收窄 1.4 个百分点。分项看，商
品零售同比增长 0.3%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；餐饮收入同比增长
0.7%，增速收窄 1.4 个百分点，整体消费需求仍偏弱。分品类看，汽车类零售
额同比下降 21.4%，降幅较上月扩大 0.2 个百分点，成为社零增长的主要拖累，
反映居民大额耐用品消费意愿不足。通讯器材类零售同比增长 6.5%，增速较
上月大幅回落 24.6 个百分点，说明前期高增长后基数效应明显，补贴效应和
换机需求均有所减弱。金银珠宝类零售同比下降 28.7%，降幅较上月扩大 19.1
个百分点，主要因近期金价回调压低零售额读数，价格因素大于需求因素，也
反映出金银珠宝消费对金价波动高度敏感。石油及制品类零售同比由下降转为

上升 4.4%，主要受近期油价上涨叠加基数因素影响。家用电器和音像器材类
零售同比上升 2.5%，家具类零售同比下降 2.6%，增速均边际回升，或受去年
“以旧换新”退潮带来的低基数影响。尽管消费总量增速有所回落，消费结构
升级和服务消费韧性仍在延续。1-10 月，社会消费品和服务零售总额同比增长
2.5%，其中服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，
以旧换新政策边际效应减弱、居民消费意愿回升偏慢等因素或持续拖累社零整
体增速。年内消费韧性更多取决于服务消费能否延续较快增速，以及即时零售、
银发经济等新增长点能否对冲传统商品消费偏弱的拖累，托举社零中枢维持在
温和区间。
 风险提示：政策落地节奏不及预期风险，海外经济超预期波动风险。已公布的
宏观或产业政策在实际执行过程中，可能因配套措施不完善、部门协调不畅、
地方执行力度差异或市场环境变化等因素，导致其推进速度、覆盖范围或最终
效果慢于或弱于市场预期，从而影响政策目标的实现与经济修复的进程；主要
经济体的政治局势、经济数据或宏观政策出现意外变化，其幅度或频率超出市

场普遍预测。这种超预期波动可能引发全球金融市场动荡、贸易条件恶化或外
需急剧收缩，并通过金融、贸易及预期渠道向国内传导，增加我国外部环境的
复杂性与不稳定性。

分析师承诺 
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明 
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准，新三板市场以三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。 
公司评级 
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上 
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-10%与 10%之间 
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-20%与-10%之间 
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-20%以下 
行业评级 
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%以下

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 政治風險, 聽證會與法案: 黃仁勳赴國會及參議院聽證, 暗示短期難以回歸; 年底NDAA法案包裹一系列
晶片相關限制等小法案, 恐進一步壓抑海外需求並加速供應鏈分化。
• 輝達量化緊縮, 連鎖效應擴散: 輝達明年營收增長率至75%, 且採購承諾將如期進行。不過新興雲端與
規模較小公司恐面臨較嚴格融資擔保條件。
• Fed助美元, 亞幣呈分化: 國際美元強勢, 但亞幣走出自己的路, 出口暢旺卻強弱分歧, 台日雙疲, 中韓強
勢, 估計該現象將延續至年底。
2. 後續看點:
• APEC會談結果(11/20)、美國PCE(11/25), OPEC+會議(11/29)。
策略思維
1) 股市−增速放緩, 但成長格局未變: 市場對AI投資效益及資料中心建置疑慮升溫, 科技股大幅修正。然而, 目
前主要瓶頸來自供給, 而非需求轉弱; 預計2028年EPS減速而不致負成長, 震盪放大但長多行情不致改變。
2) 債市−高利中樞環境成為常態: 升息預期、財政紀律及資金環境趨緊, 均將影響利率環境higher for longer; 惟

在市場波動加劇、高利優勢背景下, 固定收益資產配置價值仍高, 報酬以現金流為主。
3) 匯市−短線美元偏強震盪: 升息預期帶動美元指數走強, Fed官員偏鷹基調亦將對美元形成支撐。若未來地緣
溢價風險能延續降溫、油價走勢緩步回落, 將限縮美元指數大幅上行空間。

AI投資驅動半導體市場規模擴大 市場擔憂2028年投資放緩, 半導體銷售動能也將下滑
註: 8大CSP包括谷歌、微軟、亞馬遜、Meta、甲骨文、SpaceX、CoreWeave、Nebius。
2026年全球半導體銷售為今年前9月數據年化後結果。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/17。
• 民主黨勝選後, 市場開始擔憂AI監管政策可能影響資料中心興建進度, 如今輝達代理執行長又表示將更加審慎檢視
未來AI供應鏈投資方案, 進一步加深市場對AI資本支出放緩的疑慮。從半導體市場規模來看, 近年AI投資熱潮已帶
動產業快速擴張, 全球半導體銷售額從2025年的約7,250億美元, 預估攀升至2026年超過1.3兆美元, 顯示AI已成為
推動半導體產業成長的重要引擎。
• 然而, 市場真正擔憂的並非今年半導體需求是否持續成長, 而是2028年之後的成長動能能否延續。隨著大型CSP業
者逐步檢視資本支出效益, 加上資料中心興建可能受到融資成本與監管政策影響, 未來AI投資增速存在放緩風險,

進而使半導體銷售成長面臨壓力。

Cathay’s View
輝達的財務承諾及融資平台 市場擔心AI基建放緩, 股價評價下修
資料來源：Bloomberg,  截至2026/11/16。
• 輝達更換執行長後, 市場最關注的焦點之一, 就是先前承諾的AI相關融資計畫是否能夠持續推進, 包括已承諾的合
約, 以及規模高達5,000億美元的融資平台能否如期落實。一旦新任經營團隊對資本配置採取更審慎的態度, 相關
融資與投資承諾可能逐步放緩, 直接的影響就是資料中心的擴建速度將同步放緩。輝達最新財報中顯示, 財務承諾
金額從上季的3660億美元, 提高至5180億美元, 仍將如期進行。
• 對金融市場而言, 資料中心建置放緩, 也將導致市場下修AI需求的成長潛力, 估值也面臨同步下修的壓力, 後續將持
續關注新任執行長對投資策略及新國會後的政府動向。不過, 值得注意的是, 輝達放緩AI投資, 反而有助於輝達債
信品質的提升。
AI融資｜ 輝達目前仍維持既有融資策略, 但關注未來新任執行長的意向
資料來源：輝達財報Q3財報發布會, 截至2026/11/17。

### Page 8

8
Cathay’s View
• 新任執行長表示, 將對新興雲(Neocloud)給予「有紀律、具選擇性」的支持, 業者須先自行取得融資、完成算力部
署, 才能取得輝達320億美元的擔保承諾。預估2027年新興雲資本支出900-1000億美元, 其中CoreWeave和
Nebius預估明年的資本支出將達850億美元, 並已表示將透過客戶訂金、長約及GPU向銀行抵押融資, 取得7-8成
以上資金。其他新興雲規模較小, 即使無法取得輝達擔保承諾, 對明年整體高達1.5兆美元的AI資本支出影響有限。
• 輝達放緩AI投資, 對輝達營收和股票評價面是利空, 但對債信反而是正面影響, 輝達CDS在黃仁勳辭職後出現回落,
預期資金將流向大型CSP, 加速形成算力寡占市場。新興雲融資仰賴客戶長約與GPU抵押價值, 因此GPU租金維持
上漲、算力需求保持強勁成長, 將是未來能否降低AI融資風險的因素。

悲觀情境: 台積電2028年預估獲利從34%減至個位數 台積電啟動長期擴廠計畫, 反映對AI需求長期看好

資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 預估範圍為2027-2028年, 截至2026/11/18。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 預估範圍為2027-2028年, 截至2026/11/18。
• 市場對CSP資本支出增速放緩已形成初步共識, 並開始反映2028年半導體獲利下修風險。預估台積電2027年獲利
年增34%, 2028年受CSP投資放緩影響, EPS年增率將由26%降至個位數, 全年EPS預估160元。以11/19收盤價
2100元計算, 前瞻本益比僅約13倍, 處於歷史低檔, 股價拉回宜以買方思維看待。然而, 其他台股電子族群仍面臨
輝達供應鏈重組、零組件供需及漲價預期修正等壓力, 估值落底仍需時間, 短期震盪及反彈壓力較大。
• 台積電將赴美國德州擴廠, 並重申AI需求具備多年強勁成長動能, 反映對2030年後AI長期需求仍具信心。此外,

CSP於2028年適度放緩投資, 有助於延長AI產業成長週期, 並不代表半導體需求趨勢反轉。

增加, 記憶體股上漲動能放緩, 不過AI長期發展仍帶動獲利成長, 股價拉回接近4月起漲點可站買方思考。

近期聯準會官員談話 市場升息預期上修, 已積極預估至明年升逾3碼
註: 摘錄今年投票委員談話。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/18。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/17。
• 聯準會維持鷹派立場: 除理事Waller考慮12月升息50bps、聖路易斯Fed總裁Musalem支持2027年春季前多次升息
之外, 多數官員仍支持循序漸進的緊縮, 料12月升息1碼仍為基準情境。主席Warsh則探討AI發展的可能性, 儘管AI
效益轉化仍有待驗證, 但長期正向成長趨勢明確, 並將在未來一段期間內持續推升通膨壓力。
• 市場已提前反映未來升息空間: 現行市場預期Fed至2027年Q3累計升息3~4碼, 反映通膨黏著及經濟韌性。惟若能
源價格續跌或供需狀況緩步改善, 終端利率預估值或有調整空間。
• 高利環境持續成為常態: 通膨方面, 撇除地緣衝突影響外, 2027年AI投資動能仍將支撐經濟成長; 而美國財政赤字

及AI基礎建設再融資均推升公債/信用債供給量, 預期長天期利率續居高位。
美國利率｜官員談話延續鷹派立場, 升息預期持續支撐美債利率高檔
官員 公開談話
Michelle Bowman
(副主席, 11/10)
經濟、就業市場仍具韌性, 現階段通膨風險仍高, 而
預計2027年通膨壓力將減弱。
Christopher Waller
(理事, 11/10)
若核心CPI月增持續維持在0.3%以上, 12月應考慮一
次升息50bps, 目前限制性不足。
Kevin Warsh
(主席, 11/16)
AI發展情境推估:
1)推升經濟成長、生產力, 企業與勞工共同受益。
2)經濟活動擴張, 但正面效益與大規模失業抵銷。
3)AI發展未達預期, 引發股市下跌及經濟放緩。
Beth Hammack
(克里夫蘭, 11/16) 傾向於緩慢、穩步推進的方式小幅升息。
John Williams
(紐約, 11/18) 經濟勢頭增強、通膨居高不下, 但無需大幅升息。

ECB接班人傾向 2027/2歐美升息預期對比
資料來源: Econotream, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/17。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/10。
歐美匯率｜ECB接班對比Fed偏緊態度
• 拉加德人事變動短期雖不致改變ECB的年底升息路徑, 但因呼聲較高的接任者, 目前是偏鴿的前西班牙總裁, 加以
歐元區內部(法國預算)的金融穩定正成為限制ECB明年2月後持續升息的煞車。此外立場向來強硬的拉脫維亞總裁
卡扎克斯日前都開始強調「成長代價」與「微妙權衡」, 這向市場傳遞了一個重要訊號, 12月升息後, 利率將觸及
或略高於中性利率區間(1.75%～2.50%)上限, 2027年初若要再繼續升息, 門檻將會明顯提高。
• 對比美元受惠於美國第三季GDP成長3.4%的強勁韌性及黏著通膨, 支撐聯準會維持鷹派; 反觀歐元區第三季 GDP
僅增 0.4%, 加上法國債市風險, 歐美貨幣政策在2027年初的預期落差, 正壓抑短期歐元走勢, 支持美元高位偏強。

美元兌四國匯率走勢 四國出口年增率

• 中、韓、日、台四國近期受惠於全球貿易與AI科技需求, 出口表現普遍暢旺, 但匯率走勢卻呈現分化。
• 台日雙疲: 資金外流與政策掣肘而陷入「出口旺、匯率弱」的背離困境。新台幣受到外資在台股大舉獲利了結並

GDP意外萎縮削弱升息底氣, 加上市場對日本財務省干預漸消, 美日利差套利交易再度壓抑日圓表現。
• 中韓雙強: 韓元稱霸主要受惠出口改善, 於韓國企業響應公司治理改革, 大舉實施庫藏股並將海外盈餘與貨款匯回
國內, 形成強勁的實質換匯需求; 人民幣則在出口高成長帶動下, 受惠於中國人民銀行順應市場供需「放手」調升
中間價, 而這些因素並非單一事件而是持續發生, 後續四個貨幣的走法也隨其持續機會較大。

• 輝達整體財報優於預期：2026年Q3 (2026/7/26-2026/10/25)營收再創新高, 從前期的962億美元提升至1158億美
元(預期1090億美元), GAAP EPS從2.46美元提高至2.70美元, 毛利率為74.8%, 均優於預期。展望2026年
Q4(2026/10/25-2027/1/31), 預期營收將達1300億美元, 不過受到記憶體價格上漲影響, 毛利率受到擠壓而下滑至
71.5%, 但後續有望改善。
• 從產品別來看, 資料中心仍是營收成長的主要驅動力, 從前期的890億美元上升至1076億美元, 年增率達110%, 營
收佔比維持在9成。另外, 輝達上修明年的營收成長預估, 從70%提高至75%。

美國零售銷售和控制組月增率 消費類股強弱與標普指數
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10, 虛線為滾動3ma。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/19。
美國消費股｜市場吹起避險風, 資金流向核心消費
• 近期核心零售展現韌性的背景下, 必需消費類股在當前市場中展現出優勢。全美汽油均價略下滑, 省下的可支配現
金重新導向日常剛性採買。帶動10月核心零售銷售月增0.6%、年增5.6%, 表現優於整體零售數據, 這些日用品等
領域為必需消費產業奠定基礎。
• 另一方面, 當前市場正處於風險趨避氛圍, 高估值AI科技成長股因資本支出檢視而震盪加劇, 在「基本面直接受惠
油價下跌」與「低Beta避險屬性」的雙重加持下, 消費類股成為當前進可攻、退可守的優勢配置。

阿里巴巴營收表現 中港股大盤與阿里巴巴股價走勢
中國｜阿里財報不如預期, 黃仁勳下台衝擊「外訓內推」模式, 拖累大盤
• 阿里巴巴財報揭曉, 營收弱於預期, 印證電商「內需逆風」的窘境: 1)傳統核心電商: 「淘天集團」受制於內需疲弱,
呼應社融與消費數據陰霾, 平台營收成長力道不強; 2) AI相關的「阿里雲」: 具抗週期韌性, 因為企業為了生存反而
迫切導入AI來「降本增效」, 加上雲端系統具備高轉換成本與訂閱制鎖定慣性, 成為財報中少數亮點。
• 美國禁售高階AI晶片, 使阿里採取「外訓內推」的灰色突圍模式。 也就是在東南亞資料中心租用高階算力完成大
模型預訓練, 再將模型用國產晶片進行日常推理服務, 藉此在技術上緊咬國際巨頭。但隨黃仁勳下台、美國恐關閉

第三地算力通道, 恐讓大模型研發進度延遲, 股價單日重挫近7%。展望後市, 在財報不如預期與算力受阻的雙重夾
擊下, 阿里股價目前仍是弱勢格局。此外, 大規模財政刺激暫時無望, 長期均線空頭排列下, A股大盤目前也尚未逆
轉弱勢格局。

市場對信用審視趨於嚴謹, CCC利差持續走擴 AI融資需求仍推升信用債供給
註: 信用利差BB=182bps、B=311bps、CCC=1083bps。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/16。
註: 1)採總發行(Gross Issuance); 截至2026/11/5, hyperscalers已發行2960億美元。
2)預估IG/HY總發行: 2026年2.3兆美元/3400億美元; 2027年2.4兆美元/3750億美元。
資料來源: 高盛預估, 截至2026/11。
• 隨利率中樞上移及融資成本攀升, 低評級企業再融資壓力明顯增加, CCC利差持續高於1000bps, YTD擴張幅度明

顯高於BB及B級。然而, 目前整體HY利差約320bps,仍明顯低於歷次衰退前夕常見的500~700bps區間。反映市場
對利率水準升高的重新定價, 而非出現對硬著陸的系統性擔憂。
• 未來供給壓力仍存, 所幸增幅有望放緩: Hyperscalers發債規模持續增加, 預估2027年發行量將升破4000億美元,
持續形成信用供給壓力; 然而, 隨資料中心建設成長高峰逐步趨緩, 2028年AI資本支出增速有望降溫, 亦有助緩和市
場對現金流的疑慮。在市場已提前反映供給壓力下, 且未來有望增速趨緩前提下, 預期信用利差將維持高檔震盪,
而非持續大幅走擴。配置上, 建議優先布局基本面穩健之IG以及HY中的BB發行人, 並審慎看待CCC券種。

## Document 140: 261114_gs_lotes

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Lotes released its 3Q26 results on Nov 12th and held a results 
call on Nov 13th. Key takeaways from management include: 
(1) Lotes expects GM to begin recovering starting from 4Q26 along 
with the ramp up of AMD Venice CPU, with 4Q26 GM expected to 
reach similar levels as 2Q26 (GM was 44.8% in 3Q26, down from 
45.5% in 2Q26), and expects 4Q26 revenue to see low single digit 
QoQ growth. 
(2) Management is positive on the outlook for its server business in 
2027, expecting YoY growth to accelerate compared with 2026 
(server business with 57% YoY in 2026E per GSe), and expects 
server business to account for 60%+ of total 2027 revenue (vs. 54% 
in 2026E per GSe). The solid growth will be driven by (1) server unit 
growth of 10-15% in 2027 (up from ~10% in 2026), (2) ASP increase 
across new server CPU products (including server CPU sockets, PCIe 
cables and connectors), and (3) rising contribution from new 
AI-related products (including QD, SOCAMM and new NPO 
solutions). 
(3) The company is conservative on its 2027 GM outlook, indicating 
that overall GM is unlikely to reach the 50%+ level, mainly due to a 
higher contribution from margin-dilutive products (QD and 
SOCAMM) and delay in the ramp up of next gen server CPU products 
(MP timing for Intel Oak Stream has been delayed and will begin by 
late 1Q27 or early 2Q27). 
(4) Despite QD being margin dilutive (QD with 35-40% GM per GSe), 
management is positive that this product will drive revenue/EPS 
growth going forward. Management indicated that QD contributed 
~2% of 3Q26 revenue (with 150k+ pieces of monthly shipment per 
GSe vs. ~15k pieces of shipment in 2025). Management expects 
revenue contribution from QD will increase to 3-5% by December 
and further expand to ~10% in 2027. UTR for QD is currently full, 
and the company plans to double the capacity over the coming

### Page 3

quarters.
(5) The company expects NPO products to enter MP in 2027, although their revenue
contribution is likely to be <2%. Management anticipates revenue contribution to
increase by multiple folds in 2028, driven by broader adoption of 6.4T NPO products,
which are expected to have higher ASP than the mainstream 3.2T NPO products in 2027.
(6) For the CPU platform, Lotes mentioned Eagle Stream’s penetration is currently at
~50% (was 40-50% in 2Q26), while Birch Stream’s penetration is trending upward to

~50% (was ~40% in 2Q26). For AMD, SP5 remains mainstream while current penetration
for Venice is at <3%, and is expected to reach 3-5% by 2026 end. The company now
expects AMD Venice penetration to average 20% in 2027 (previously expect to reach
30-40% by 2027 end) while Intel’s Oak Stream is expected to reach an average
penetration of 10-15% in 2H27 (previously expect to reach 20-30% by 2027 end).
Investment view
We remain relatively cautious about Lotes’ near term business outlook, and we expect

limited room for meaningful margin improvement in the near term, mainly due to (1)
CPU socket shipments remain heavily focused toward legacy products, as the ramp up of
next gen CPU platforms (including AMD Venice and Intel Oak Stream CPU) continues to
face delays, and we believe the delays are partly due to potential shortages of key
components across the general server supply chain (including ABF substrate, memory,
PCB/CCL, etc), (2) growing contribution from margin-dilutive products starting from

2H26 (including QD and SOCAMM, which accounted for 4-5% of total 3Q26 revenue per
GSe), and (3) rising material cost could weigh on profitability as the company has limited
pricing power to fully pass on cost increases without risking market share.
However, we remain constructive on the outlook for general servers (we now expect
general server volume to increase by 20%/12%/8% YoY in 2026E-28E, was 14%/6%/6%
previously, see here), which we believe Lotes is the key beneficiary with its solid market

share in the server CPU socket market. We expect Lotes’ server revenue to see 38%
2026-28E CAGR (tracking above the 28% CAGR seen during the previous high-growth
server cycle in 2019-22), and we continue to believe server is the key margin expansion
driver given its above corporate average margin (general server socket GM at ~60% vs.
3Q26 total GM of 45%) and >55% revenue contribution throughout 2026E-28E.
Overall, we believe Lotes’ current share price has largely priced in the positive outlook

for general servers (the stock is trading at 16x+ 2027E P/E based on GSe/BBG
consensus, which is +2 STDV above the average P/E during the previous high growth
server cycle in 2019-22). The revenue contribution from higher margin new products
(including next gen CPU sockets, GPU sockets, and NPO products) is likely to remain
limited in the near-term.
We maintain our Neutral rating and lower our 12m TP to NT$1,640 (from NT1,670).
3Q26 overview
Lotes’ 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 23%/15% lower than GSe and BBG consensus, with

GM 1.6ppt/1.0ppt lower than GSe/BBG consensus, which was driven by higher material
costs and higher contribution from new AI-related products with lower margins. The
OPEX to sales ratio was 2.7ppt/2.4ppt higher than GSe and BBG consensus, due to
increasing R&D spending and labor costs. For earnings, Lotes’ NI was 18%/15% lower

than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by the lower than expected GM.

Earnings revisions: 
We revise down our 2026E revenue by 5% to factor in 3Q26 actual results and revise 
down our 2027E revenue by 1% to factor in delay in new CPU socket products, but revise 
up our 2028E/2029E revenue estimates by 2%/2% to factor in the 
better-than-expected contribution from the server business. We revise down our 
2026/27/28/29E GM estimates by 1.2/2.2/1.2/0.9ppt to factor in the ongoing rising 
material costs and delay in new CPU socket products. Overall, we revise down our 
2026/27/28E earnings estimates by 11/8/1%. 
Valuation:  
Maintain Neutral rating and revise down our 12-m TP to NT$1,640 (from NT$1,670), 
based on an unchanged P/E of 16.0x (which is at the company’s average valuation since 
2020) and applied to 2027E EPS (rolled over from 4Q26-3Q27E), and factor in our 
earnings revisions.

### Page 5

Lotes is a connector supplier mainly focusing on the niche CPU socket market with more 
than 40% share in 2024, but we see pressure for its market share outlook in the coming 
years considering the intensifying competition in the server CPU market. We maintain 
our positive view on the company’s GM expansion potential longer term, which we 
believe will be mainly driven by better product mix (Venice server CPU to be released in 
2H26 and Oak Stream server CPU to be released in 2027, both with better ASP and 
margin). Meanwhile, Lotes’ CPU socket product line’s ASP/GM are higher than the 
company average, and we see scope for both to grow even faster in the new product 
generation given the larger socket size and pin count required. However, the company’s 
new product line, Quick Disconnects (UQD or MQD) for liquid cooling systems, is likely to 
deliver very limited revenue/gross profit contribution in the coming years (0.6/1.2% 
revenue and 0.4/0.8% GP contribution in 2026E/2027E) given thermal solution 
providers typically ship cold plates as integrated modules and leading thermal suppliers 
tend to secure QD supply in-house or through affiliated suppliers. We are Neutral rated 
on Lotes considering the share loss risk in server sockets and the shares trading at the 
historical peak P/E level, making valuation less attractive, despite the company’s solid 
revenue/GM expansion trend driven by socket upgrading demand. Key risks: (1) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected server shipment growth which would raise/lower 
server CPU socket demand; (2) lower-/higher-than-expected component costs without 
product pricing adjustment; (3) slower-/faster-than-expected AI server-related 
contribution.

Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,640 is based on a target 
P/E of 16.0x (in line with the company’s average valuation since 2020) applied to our 
2027E EPS.  
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Stronger-/weaker-than-expected server CPU demand, 
(2) lower-/higher-than-expected component costs, and (3) 
slower-/faster-than-expected AI server-related contribution.

return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

M
Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Component supply to improve: While component supply could stay tight near
term, Delta expects to secure bigger volume in the coming quarters, so it should
have better order fulfillment ahead. Oct-26 monthly sales seems to point to double-
digit% QoQ revenue growth in 4Q26, in our view, and it is likely to see better than
seasonal growth in 1Q27. It expects the component supply tightness to start easing

in 2Q27.
HVDC contribution minor  near term: It should start to ship HVDC power racks in
4Q26, but the contribution should be relatively small. For the GPU platform,
scheduled for 2H27, Delta sees 80VDC as the only reference design as of now,
although it does not seem to be finalized yet. Our conversations indicate most
investors do not have that as the base case, so there could be upside to consensus
estimates in 2028, if that ends up being the case.

Fuel cell another driver longer term: Delta will have some pilot production in late
2026. Its BoD has approved a greenfield project for the fuel cell business. This fits
well into its power delivery business, especially amid the power constraints that
hyperscalers are being confronted with now.

Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 195: 4万亿“灰飞烟灭”、疫情以来最惨烈的一周！美国科技股跌入熊市

Source PDF: all report pdf/4万亿“灰飞烟灭”、疫情以来最惨烈的一周！美国科技股跌入熊市.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-21
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美国科技股遭遇新冠疫情以来最惨烈⼀周，纳斯达克 100 指数跌⼊熊市，本周科技股市值蒸
发逾 4 万亿美元。 Anthropic 上市⾸⽇涨幅迅速回落，英伟达因财务丑闻单⽇暴跌 14% ，拖
累芯⽚板块崩盘。恐慌指数 VIX ⼀度飙升⾄ 40 点，⽐特币跌破 6 万美元，⻩⾦下挫近 3% ，
投资者涌⼊美债避险， 10 年期收益率降⾄ 4.94% 附近。
摘要：
美国科技股遭遇新冠疫情暴发以来最惨烈的⼀周， AI 投资热潮降温引发的恐慌情绪席卷华尔
街。
周五，纳斯达克 100 指数单⽇重挫 5.6% ，当周累计下跌 10.4% ，创逾六年来最⼤单周跌幅，较 10
⽉ 6 ⽇盘中⾼点累计下跌已逾 20% ，正式步⼊熊市区间。本周科技股市值蒸发逾 4 万亿美元。
到周五收盘，纳指抹平今年内累计涨幅转跌
标普 500 指数同期下跌 3.7% ，较 10 ⽉⾼点回调超过 10% ，进⼊技术性调整区域。
⽆论是业绩和指引超预期强劲的英伟达财报，还是 Anthropic 创纪录的 IPO 募资，本周都未能拯

救美股
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

与此同时， Anthropic 上市⾸⽇⼤涨 17% 的喜悦昙花⼀现，次⽇即告回落，这家 AI 明星公司的
IPO ⾮但未能提振市场信⼼，反⽽成为本轮抛售加速的导⽕索之⼀。
市场恐慌情绪迅速蔓延⾄⻛险资产全线。华尔街" 恐慌指数 "VIX⼀度飙升⾄ 40 点，创 2025 年 4 ⽉
以来最⾼⽔平；⽐特币跌破 6 万美元，创两年新低；⻩⾦现货价格下挫近 3% ⾄每⾦衡盎司约
3522 美元，触及逾⼀年低位；
本周，⻩⾦暴跌 9%
投资者争相涌⼊避险资产， 10 年期美国国债收益率下⾏ 10 个基点⾄ 4.94% 。
10 年期美国国债收益率下⾏⾄ 5% 下⽅
纳斯达克 100 创六年最差单周表现，正式步⼊熊市
据英国《⾦融时报》报道，纳斯达克 100 指数周五单⽇重挫 5.6% ，周跌幅扩⼤⾄ 10.4% ，为逾六
年来最差单周表现。该指数较 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇盘中⾼点累计下跌 20% ，正式触及熊市技术⻔槛。

### Page 3

标普 500 指数周五下跌 3.7% ，⾃ 10 ⽉峰值以来跌幅已超 10% ，进⼊调整区间，⾃ 4 ⽉份以来⾸次
收于 200 ⽇移动平均线以下。道琼斯⼯业平均指数本周跌幅达 8% 。
据华尔街⽇报报道，本周纳斯达克综合指数成分股合计蒸发市值逾 4 万亿美元，该指数今年以来
的全部涨幅已告抹平。 " 七⼤科技巨头 " ⽆⼀幸免，新兴云计算公司和芯⽚制造商普遍出现两位数
跌幅。

### Page 4

这场抛售的根源在于市场对 AI 投资狂潮可持续性的深层质疑。⼤型科技公司为建设芯⽚和数据
中⼼⽽⼤举举债投⼊，但 AI 相关企业的营收增速正在放缓。
据彭博智库数据， AI 企业在标普 500 中的销售增速预计将从第三季度的 35% 降⾄明年底的 25% 以
下，⽽其现⾦流与资本⽀出之⽐已跌⾄三年前的约五分之⼀。
据摩根⼠丹利投资管理⾸席投资官 Jim Caron 判断，⺠主党在本⽉中期选举中的⼤胜 " 绝对 " 是加
速这轮市场抛售的因素之⼀。他说：
在货币政策层⾯，美联储的加息预期持续压制市场情绪。美联储 9 ⽉已时隔三年重启加息，市场
原本对 12 ⽉再次加息 25 个基点的预期出现动摇，但美联储理事 Christopher Waller 此前曾放⻛⼀
次性加息 50 个基点。
Nomura 美洲股票联席主管 Alex Witten 指出：
Anthropic IPO 次⽇⼀度跌超 10% ， AI 明星上市也没能救市
周四上市的 Anthropic （ ANT ）当天收涨 17.1% ，⼀度较 83 美元的发⾏价上涨近四成，成为今年

全球资本市场最受瞩⽬的 IPO 之⼀。但上市次⽇，股价便⼤幅回落：周五收跌 8.5% ，盘中⼀度
跌⾄ 83.05 美元，⽇内跌幅超过 14% ，距离 IPO 发⾏价仅差 5 美分，险些破发。
Anthropic 股价的迅速回落，成为 AI 交易情绪转变的⼀个缩影。市场此前期待这家明星 AI 公司的
上市能够重新点燃投资者对 AI 板块的热情，但 IPO ⾸⽇的上涨并未带来持续的板块买盘。相反，
随着 Anthropic 次⽇转跌，英伟达、 AMD 及其他 AI 相关股票同步下挫。
与 AI 资本开⽀相关的所有⽀出 —— 我们知道正是这些⽀出推动了增⻓ —— 那么，这些⽀出是否会
被新⼀届国会明年就任后启动的调查和政策所压制？市场正在努⼒搞清楚这⼀点。
股市⼀直聚焦于英伟达丑闻，但现在我们同时⾯临美联储准备加息以及⼤宗商品市场反映的衰退
⻛险。

### Page 5

媒体指出， Anthropic 上市与英伟达最新季度业绩都未能打消市场对 AI ⻛险和巨额投资的担忧。
Horizon Investment Services ⾸席执⾏官 Chuck Carlson 表⽰：
这番话点出了市场的核⼼变化：投资者并⾮不相信 AI 技术前景，⽽是开始质疑当前估值是否已经
充分计⼊未来增⻓。 Anthropic 上市⾸⽇的热度没能延续，意味着即使是最受追捧的 AI 公司，也
未必能在当前市场环境中获得持续的估值溢价。
更深层的担忧在于 AI 商业化和资本开⽀之间的落差。⼤型科技公司过去⼀年投⼊巨额资⾦建设数
据中⼼、采购芯⽚和扩充算⼒，但投资者越来越关注这些投⼊何时能够转化为⾜以覆盖成本的
收⼊和利润。 AI 需求仍在增⻓，并不意味着所有相关企业都能获得与投资规模相匹配的回报。
英伟达七连跌， SEC 会计调查再添压⼒，市值较巅峰蒸发 2.65 万亿
英伟达（ NVDA ）周五收跌 14% ，本周累跌 22.92% ，创 2008 年 10 ⽉以来最⼤单周跌幅。

⾃ CEO ⻩仁勋 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇上周四离任后，英伟达股价已连续七个交易⽇下跌，也未能扭转跌势七
⽇累计跌幅达 27.74% 。即使本周⼆盘后英伟达公布的第三财季营收和第四财季指引均超预期强
劲，该司⾼管还上调了下个财年的营收增⻓指引，也未能扭转股价跌势。
截⾄周五收盘，英伟达市值较 10 ⽉接近 6 万亿美元的峰值蒸发约 2.65 万亿美元，苹果（ AAPL ）、
微软（ MSFT ）和⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet （ GOOGL ）均已超过英伟达，后者退居美国上市公司市
值第四位。
“ 英伟达（本周）公布的业绩可能是企业盈利史上最出⾊的季度表现之⼀，他们把所有事情都做
对了。我和许多投资者⼀样，原本在某种程度上期待 Anthropic 上市能够成为⼀次市场情绪的出
清事件，但它并没有起到这样的作⽤。 ”

### Page 6

英伟达⾯临的压⼒，已不仅仅是整个 AI 板块估值回调。⻩仁勋离任后，公司由⾸席财务官
（ CFO ）克雷斯（ Colette Kress ）临时兼任 CEO ，管理层更迭本⾝就令投资者对公司未来战略
和经营稳定性产⽣疑虑。与此同时，围绕⻩仁勋个⼈婚外情及其关联⼈⼠的调查报道，也持续
给公司带来声誉和监管层⾯的不确定性。
监管压⼒更在本周进⼀步升级。《华尔街⽇报》周⼆报道称，美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）正在
调查英伟达，但当时尚⽆法确定调查的具体重点。路透社周五进⼀步援引两名知情⼈⼠报道
称， SEC 执法部⻔正在仔细审查英伟达可能存在的会计问题。
路透社称，调查关注点之⼀是英伟达此前调整地理区域收⼊披露⼝径：公司改为按照客⼾总部
所在地，⽽⾮账单地址统计收⼊，调整后新加坡不再单独出现在地区收⼊披露中。与此同时，
市场也在关注英伟达参与的复杂 AI 融资安排 —— 芯⽚⼚商通过投资、担保等⽅式帮助客⼾获得资
⾦，以购买芯⽚和建设数据中⼼。这类安排可能令投资者更难判断收⼊质量和公司实际承担的

财务⻛险。
需要强调的是， SEC 调查并不代表英伟达已经存在违规⾏为，路透社也表⽰⽬前尚⽆法确认调
查何时启动、具体审查哪些会计事项。不过，监管调查在股价已经⼤幅下跌、管理层刚刚发⽣
变动之际传出，⽆疑进⼀步打击了投资者信⼼。
路透社报道发布前，英伟达股价跌幅已超过 6% ，报道传出后，股价跌幅扩⼤⾄ 10% 以上。
沽空报告后 AMD 连续两⽇重挫， AI 产业链遭全⾯抛售
半导体板块的抛售也在向其他公司扩散。 AMD （ AMD ）周四在沽空机构 Hunterbrook 发布报告
后收跌 7.7% ，周五进⼀步下跌逾 13% ，连续第⼆个交易⽇⼤幅下挫。费城半导体指数周五收跌
约 10.7% ，反映市场抛售已从个别公司扩散⾄整个芯⽚板块。
其他 AI 产业链股票同样承压。美光（ MU ）周五跌约 14% ，博通（ AVGO ）跌逾 12% ；云算⼒服
务 商 CoreWeave （ CRWV ） 和 Nebius 分 别 下 跌 约 12% 和 16% 。 ⼤ 型 科 技 公 司 中 ， Meta

（ META ）跌约 5.9% ，亚⻢逊（ AMZN ）跌约 4.1% 。
AI 基础设施投资链条也受到波及。卡特彼勒（ CAT ）周五下跌约 5% ，市场担忧如果数据中⼼建
设热潮降温，发电和备⽤电⼒设备需求也可能受到影响。换⾔之，市场正在重新评估的不只是
芯⽚⼚商的估值，还包括数据中⼼建设、电⼒供应和云算⼒租赁等⼀整条 AI 投资链的增⻓预期。
AI 资本开⽀回报遭质疑，拥挤交易平仓放⼤跌势

### Page 7

这轮抛售最重要的深层原因，是市场对 AI 投资回报周期的重新审视。过去，投资者将 AI 视为⼀场
⾜以重塑经济和企业盈利的⻓期技术⾰命，愿意提前为算⼒需求和数据中⼼建设⽀付⾼估值。
但随着全球科技巨头不断提⾼资本开⽀，市场开始追问：这些投⼊究竟能够创造多少新增收
⼊？利润何时兑现？如果商业化进度不及预期，庞⼤的算⼒基础设施⼜将由谁来承担成本？
彭博社将这轮⾏情描述为 AI 交易的快速重新定价：投资者开始重新评估数万亿美元 AI 投资未来可
能带来的利润，⽽不再单纯追逐资本开⽀增⻓本⾝。 Anthropic 上市次⽇回落，也让市场意识
到，单个 AI 企业获得巨额融资和⾼估值，并不能证明整个⾏业的投资逻辑已经得到验证。
与此同时， AI 相关股票此前涨幅巨⼤，交易也相对拥挤。当英伟达等⻰头股持续下跌，投资者可
能被迫削减相关仓位、降低杠杆，进⽽引发更多抛售。半导体指数、云算⼒服务商和⼤型科技
股同步⼤跌，正说明这已不是个别公司的基本⾯调整，⽽是市场整体降低 AI ⻛险敞⼝的过程。
美国银⾏财富管理公司⾼级投资总监 Bill Northey 表⽰：

加息押注升温，估值压⼒与避险情绪形成共振
宏观环境也在放⼤ AI 股票的压⼒。美国通胀数据⾼于预期后，市场对美联储 12 ⽉会议加息 25 个
基点的押注升⾄接近确定。美联储理事库克（ Lisa Cook ）周五表⽰，当前政策利率还不⾜以应
对通胀⽔平。下周公布的美联储⻘睐通胀指标个⼈消费⽀出（ PCE ）数据，将成为市场判断联
储政策路径的重要依据。
利率预期变化对 AI 股票尤其不利。 AI 企业的⾼估值很⼤程度上建⽴在未来多年收⼊和利润快速增
⻓的预期之上；利率越⾼，远期现⾦流折现价值越低，投资者愿意为尚未兑现的增⻓⽀付的价
格也就越低。同时，数据中⼼建设和算⼒扩张⾼度依赖融资，融资成本上升也会增加 AI 投资项⽬
的资⾦压⼒。
周五，美股⼤跌伴随美债收益率明显回落， 10 年期美债收益率⽇内降约 11 个基点⾄ 4.94% 附
近，显⽰资⾦正在转向相对安全的资产。 VIX 恐慌指数⼤幅上升，市场避险情绪进⼀步升温。
“ 但如果我们退⼀步，从基本⾯和市场情绪两个⻆度审视，今天显然是市场情绪占据了上⻛。 ”

“ 短期内，情绪始终是更有⼒量的驱动因素；但从更⻓时间跨度看，最终占上⻛的通常还是基本
⾯。 ”

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI.
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy.
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the

was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.
